Kara narrowed her eyes, before pulling her sister into a hug. “You need sleep,” she murmured.

“You do too,” Alex replied, shifting until Kara let her head fall to her shoulder.  

They stood there for a minute, not needing to say a thing. When Kara stepped back, Alex’s pajama bottoms brushed against her boots. She rolled her eyes at her sister’s overprotective tendencies but didn’t shrug off the hand still rubbing soothing circles over her suit at her shoulder.

They slowly made their way to the couch, Kara too exhausted to go through the motions of changing out of her suit.

“They were the same woman?” Alex asked after Kara had updated her on tonight’s events.

Kara nodded, wrapping the end of her cape around her fingers.

“So, what did you do after you saw her?”

Kara avoided her sister’s eyes and continue to play with her cape, anxiously.

“You came here after?” Alex guessed.

Kara shook her head no. “I actually flew around for twenty minutes first,” she replied, barely above a whisper.

Alex nudged her shoulder until Kara finally looked up.

“What?” she said, feigning hurt and shoving her sister’s hand away.

“Kara, you need to call her.”

Kara shook her head, adamantly.

“No. I already decided that was a bad idea.”

“And what? Ignoring her now is a good one?”

Kara groaned and leaned forward, bracing her head in her hands.

“Here,” Alex said.

She was holding Kara’s cell phone in her hand. Kara could just make out the reflective screen underneath the curtain of hair surrounding her face.

“When did you take that?” Kara said, trying to sound indignant.

When Alex didn’t waver, she took it begrudgingly, and unlocked the screen.

Once faced with Lena’s contact info, Kara said, “I think I should wait and call her in the morning.”

One look to Alex told her that was not going to happen.

“Okay, okay.”

She sighed and sat back against the couch cushions, before calling Lena’s number and waiting patiently. On the third ring, the speaker crackled, and a voice broke through the quiet.

“Hello?” Lena said.

Kara barely registered the greeting, so overwhelmed by the gravely purr of her voice. After the possibly world-shifting shock wore off, Kara looked up to see Alex staring back at her with wild eyes.

Speak, Alex mouthed.

“Hi,” Kara said, her voice hoarse. She cleared it before adding, “Lena.”

There was another pause, before Lena replied, “I stepped out for a bit to grab a bottle of kombucha, but I should be back later.”

What?

Kara moved the phone away from her ear and switched it to speaker phone.

Before Kara had time to reply, Lena continued, “I understand, but if you need more info about the Lego issue, you can reach out to Clark.”

Clark? Lego?

“Lena?” Kara tried, holding the phone’s mic close to her mouth.

There was another extended pause where only static filled the silence, then, “He apparently submitted some garbage on Tuesday and now we’re all going to be in trouble if he doesn’t get on it like, now.”

Kara’s mind was reeling. Before she got a chance to say anything else, Lena had said goodbye, and the line went dead.

“What was that about?” Alex asked, leaning closer from her spot on the couch.

Kara tucked her phone back into the side of her boot and shook her head.

“I—I don’t…” she trailed off as the she ran through Lena’s words over and over in her head. “I’m not sure.”

Alex nodded, before she sat up straight and turned fully towards Kara. “You don’t think she knows another Clark, do you?”

17. Lena

Summary for the Chapter:

A final confrontation, the beginning of the end, and finally, finally, Lena and Kara talk. Sort of.

Notes for the Chapter:

Crosses fingers I'll keep up this motivation to push to the end now!

Thank you for sticking with this story over the past six years (yikes...)

Lena Kieran Luthor, according to her mother, had been and always would be a failure. She’d entered Lillian’s life as a dark mark on the Luthor name and nothing she did ever seemed to erase what would forever be indelible. If only she had known back then that all of her attempts to change that would be pointless; maybe she could have stopped trying long before she cut ties.

Now, sitting across from Jack as she attempted a world record for longest time to sip one cup of room-temperature tea, she had to agree with Lillian. She was a failure.

“Tell me what’s giving you reservations, Lena,” Jack said.

He was leaning over the table now, so close he could reach out and grasp her chin in one single movement. Lena had the feeling that it would not be so romantic this time around if he did so.

“I can’t move back to that city,” Lena reiterated for maybe the fourth time.

A complete aversion to moving back to Metropolis was her one saving grace at this point, for every other grievance she’d voiced, Jack had had a solution for. Lena sat back in her chair, taking her nearly empty cup with her. She cradled it in both hands, holding onto it tight as if it were still warm and comforting.

Lena saw Jack’s face flicker from confusion, to rage, and then back to complacency in a matter of seconds. He tore his gaze away from her, observing the other people in the café—mostly students by the looks of it.

“I think,” he began a moment later, voice low and steely, “that after a bit of persuading, you’d come to appreciate Metropolis again. Biospheer too, has really changed since you left.”

His face was calm, but there was an icy quality to his voice that gave Lena the impression she was being threatened.

“I’d be hard pressed to believe that,” she replied, trying to add a hint of playful banter to her inflection.

She clearly fell short.

“I don’t think you have a choice.”

Momentarily stupefied by the intensity behind his eyes, she suddenly felt her cup leave her hands. When she looked up, Jack was standing beside her.

“I see you’re finished with your drink. Let me toss it in the bin for you. Then we can leave.”

Lena nodded by rote. When Jack turned away, she took the brief reprieve and glanced down at her phone. The screen was still dark. No other calls from K Catco. Lena bit at the inside of her cheek and locked the phone, fingers shaking as she drew her hand away quickly.

“You ready to go?”

Could she say no? What would happen if she did?

She’d spent the last hour trying to appease him without fully agreeing to join him on whatever morally- misaligned crusade he was on; that, apparently, had only bided her a little time.

“Yes,” she said, forcing a smile. “I just need to use the restroom first and then we can go.”

He hesitated, hand gripping the back of her chair before he relented and let her stand.

“Of course. I’ll be waiting out here.”

Lena scooted the chair back and reached for her purse and phone when a strong hand caught her by the wrist.

“I can hold that for you. Who knows when they cleaned the toilets last.”

He shared an appeasing smile just as fake as her own and waved her forward as if giving her permission.

As Lena made her way towards the back hall where the single person restrooms were, she hoped, desperately, that someone might have eavesdropped on her conversation or at least taken notice of Jack’s unsettling behaviour, but no one seemed to pay her any mind. Their heads were drawn towards open notebooks and laptops plugged into the outlets underneath the circular tables. Even the baristas looked absorbed in their own work. The younger man who’d taken their order earlier flashed Lena a diplomatic smile, before emptying the bucket of ice he’d just carried in from the back.

If she were a braver individual, she might have tried to make a move now that she was out of Jack’s line of sight, but in addition to being a failure, she was also a coward.

God. The evidence had been right there under Lena’s nose the whole time. Maybe if she had cared less about herself and how Kara saw her, she could have warned Supergirl sooner and saved lives. Saved lives. People had died. It didn’t seem to matter how far she got from the Luthor name. Loss would always follow her.

With a disgusted shake of her head, Lena took a shaky breath, then went to open the door to the bathroom. When she pulled down on the handle though, it was locked. She tried again, but to no avail. Was it locked or was someone else in there now? There was a keypad to the side of the door, but no indication as to whether it was in use.

Lena stepped back and waited against the opposite wall. She heard nothing from the other side of the door, but really didn’t want to go in search of an employee for a key or the entry code if it meant facing Jack again. Just as she heard a toilet flushing, she felt a buzz at her wrist.

There was a message on her smart watch. From K Catco.

 [K Catco] On it. I promise.

Shit.

Lena tapped the message away quickly and hustled back towards the front room.

“The restroom’s free if you need it,” a voice called out from behind her.

She didn’t even look back as she rounded the corner, trying to appear as level-headed as possible. It was all for not though. Jack’s gaze was narrowed in on her phone screen. He wouldn’t have seen what the message said. Lena didn’t have message preview turned on; she wasn’t that daft. However, receiving a message at nearly two a.m. from the same number that had called her earlier was suspicious enough.

Lena tried to act as if it was nothing, but her head was pounding. The expression she saw on her once familiar friend’s face was so foreign, it made her hands twitch at her sides before she could hide them away.

Instead of approaching her, Jack simply sneered and took his own phone out from his pocket. He had it raised to his ear, no doubt ringing as he waited for an answer.

“I’m ready to go,” Lena tried, but her voice immediately felt swallowed up by the clicking of computer keys and the whir of the coffee bean grinder.

He was speaking now to someone on the other end and nodding his head slowly. Lena contemplated crossing the ten or so feet between them to knock it from his hand, when she saw a flash of red and blue over his shoulder.

Kara.

“On second thought, I think I should really head home now,” Lena said, loud enough this time to draw his attention to her.

She took off into a run, heading for the side entrance, and accidentally knocked an empty chair over with her hip. She forced herself not to look back as she heard the chair skidding loudly against the tile floor. An alarm sounded the second the door flung open and then she was outside on the street, running haphazardly in her heels. She reached the centre courtyard and was just about to stop to catch her breath when suddenly, she went down.  

Pained flared up, first at her knee and then her chin. Her hands felt like sandpaper, brushing against the tender skin of her face. She looked down at her fingers, expecting blood, but there was only a light smattering of dirt from the ground where she’d landed. She moved to sit up so she could take a look at her knee, when a crash emanated from behind her, loud enough to be heard over the alarm from the fire-exit door.

Her initial fear forced her gaze inside the café, but except for the mess from her own tear out the door, nothing was disturbed, and no people were hurt.

“You don’t need to do this,” came Supergirl’s voice over all the chaos around Lena.

It was resolute and steady, very unlike the woman Lena had talked with across a bar table only the week before.

Lena got to her knees and sank against a park bench to her right as she watched Supergirl restrain Jack against the side of the building. He didn’t even try to free himself from Supergirl at first. He ignored her, searching around him until he locked onto Lena and leered. Immediately, she tensed, wanting to disappear into the foliage at her back.

Supergirl gave Jack another shove and he eventually replied, “What I do does not concern you.”

Lena’s breathing seemed to kick into overdrive, and she sucked in a breath, watching the stalemate. There appeared to be a whole conversation going on between them from their stony looks alone.

“I assure you, Mr. Spheer, that anything concerning the safety of the people of National City, does in fact, concern me.”

Jack chuckled. “Is it just the city or are you thinking of a certain transplant, perhaps?”

Supergirl’s nostrils flared, but her rock-solid hold on his shoulders didn’t waver.

“Like I said before, you don’t need to do this.”

Supergirl’s gaze stayed trained on Jack, before it flickered Lena’s direction. For the first time in days, Lena felt like she could breathe again. Kara wasn’t mad. At least she didn’t look mad at Lena. Jack on the other hand…

Kara had since turned back to him and was still talking him down, when all of a sudden, he whispered something too quiet for Lena to hear, and Supergirl’s stance wilted. Lena wasn’t too sure what had been said, but she was pretty sure Kara’s name had left his lips. A pair of lips that had taken on a metallic sheen.

“Supergirl!” Lena shouted, but her outburst came too late.

In a blur, Supergirl was tumbling backwards, unearthing a track of pavement in her wake. Jack followed without pause, moving fast to catch her off guard again. They collided just as Supergirl got to her feet, but instead of meeting Kara’s own punch, Jack dodged and used Supergirl’s strength against her, sending her careening forwards.  

Lena tasted blood on her own tongue and released her teeth’s hold on her gums. Her whole body felt locked up as she watched the fight progress before her. Supergirl was stronger, but with the nano-cells, Jack was almost twice as fast. For every hit Kara landed, Jack was able to get in ten of his own. Thankfully, after a tiring back and forth that seemed to stretch on until morning, Supergirl began to adjust her attack, eventually cracking him sharply over the head as he tried to rush past.

Jack staggered back, almost collapsing to the ground. He braced himself, one bloodied hand against the concrete, while the other raised behind him ready to swing again. Once again, Supergirl was ready; she caught him below the shoulder blades before he was able to adjust to her new stance.

It was odd. If the assumptions Lena made back in Dilworth’s office were correct, Jack’s higher functions were under the control of the AI program Lena had coded when they’d still been working together. But the longer she watched them fight, the less sure Lena became of this theory. The nano-cells’ functions were primitive. Lena’s own code might not have been ready for use yet, but at least it learned. This code just rinsed and repeated like a perpetual loop coded by a first-year mathematics student. Not even Jack would write something so mundane.

A thought occurred to Lena, just as Jack was blindsided again.

He hadn’t needed to silence her. He needed her help. He needed her to fix her AI. That’s why he’d approached the competition. Not to eliminate them initially, but in hopes of mending Lena’s original code.

But… that left one thing up in the air. What—or more appropriately—who was controlling the nano-cells now?

Lena stood from where she was crouched and backed up until she’d made it a block away from the fight. It was slowly breaking down as Jack hit the dirt over and over again. With so many nano-cells coursing through his system, he was resilient, but not indomitable. If this didn’t end soon, there was absolutely no way he’d ever recover—if the man underneath all that tech was even still there.

Hundreds of things were happening around Lena now that she took a moment to drag her eyes away from Supergirl and Jack. The café patrons were ducked for cover, but still watching the fight unfold with intrigue. A late-night news outlet had since arrived on scene, filming from afar, and three unmarked black vans now sat idling in the street a couple blocks south.

The courtyard also appeared to be far more populated than it had been an hour earlier.

Through the mess, Lena was only concerned with one thing. Finding her.

 

The task wasn’t as easy as Lena had hoped. Though the image of the woman with dark hair from the coffee shop was ingrained in Lena’s memory, it was her possessiveness over Jack that stood out now, more than her finer features.

It didn’t help that it was the middle of the night, either. Try as hard as she could, Lena did not have the eyesight to identify her from the masses. Everyone around her was watching the fight unfold.

But wait. Maybe that was it. Nearly everybody would be watching, captivated by another battle on the streets of National City, but one person would not.

Lena changed tactics, jogging at a slow pace so her heels wouldn’t catch on the sidewalk again, and began looking for an oddity. This was her specialty: reviewing thousands of lines of code for the one section that stood out.

Lena knew there was the possibility that the woman just wouldn’t be there tonight; however, her possessiveness of Jack said otherwise. Lena had seen jealous lovers before, but the way she’d bore into Lena when the woman had interrupted their conversation on Saturday had been on a whole other level.

After passing the intersection near the unmarked vans and walking slowly past a bank a little too close to the fight for her own comfort, Lena stopped in her tracks. There, across the street from the coffee shop, a woman was sitting at a patio table for a restaurant long closed. Her eyes were glued to the tablet in her hands, sparingly glancing up at the fight.

“Gotcha, bitch,” Lena muttered to herself.

She picked up her pace, not worried about the clicking of her heels. Between the sound of a broken water hydrant and the cacophony of unsyncopated car alarms, Lena could barely hear her own thoughts as it was.

Just as she was approaching the restaurant, the woman stood with her tablet tucked under her arm and strode toward the street. When she reached the intersection, she pulled it out again, tapping incessantly, before huffing in annoyance. She kept stopping, looking down, then walking again, moving closer and closer to the fight until she stood near the entrance to the coffee shop.

“Ma’am!” one of the employees yelled, holding the door open and trying to usher her inside.

She tried waving him off, but his unwanted chivalry was relentless. He even tried grabbing for her arm when she ignored him a second time. As she shoved him away, Lena took advantage of the opening. In a few long strides, she reached the woman and was just inches from snatching the tablet when another fire hydrant burst. The spray erupted in a powerful surge, drenching Lena from the waist down, before slowing to a steady spray.

When the shock wore off, Lena looked back up to see the woman smirking at her. She hadn’t avoided the splash zone entirely but was far better off than Lena.

“So, you figured it out?”

Lena opened her mouth to answer, snippy comeback on the edge of her tongue, when she thought of an even better response. It was especially satisfying, watching the woman’s cocky smile quickly fall away as Lena’s fist sailed upward through the air, striking her just below the nose.

Lena wasn’t going to lie. It hurt like hell, but she’d remembered—just as Lex had taught her—not to clench her thumb.

As the woman keeled over, hand rushing to cover her nose, Lena grabbed for the tablet again, this time wrenching it from her grasp. There was a program open. Version 2.1, read the title at the top. Underneath, a red banner took up half of the screen, warning the user that manual controls had been disabled and would not reconnect.

 “You can’t stop this,” the woman wheezed, forcing Lena to draw her gaze away from the screen.

She was clutching her nose still, trying to stymie the blood sliding down the outside of her fingers.

“I can certainly try,” Lena bit back, before walking off to end all of this.

 

The streets outside the van passed quicker now that they’d left the downtown core. Lena pulled her gaze away from the window and studied the woman in the seat across from her. She was dressed in all black tonight, but Lena still recognized her as one of the women who’d arrived at the bar with Kara the other night.

“Is Ka—Supergirl not coming with us?” Lena’s eyes flashed from the woman to the driver up front and the other armed guard in the passenger’s seat.

At first, Lena wondered if her question would go unanswered, but when she turned, the woman was shooting her an incredulous expression.

“She will,” the woman said.

“Oh, she—” Lena paused.

Right. She can fly.

Lena nodded her head and turned away. If it weren’t for the uncertainty of the unfolding hours to come, she might have felt embarrassed, but that feeling wasn’t coming right now.

After struggling with the nano-cell program and finding no way to override the code, Lena had relented and did a forced shut down. It had been a gamble. Thankfully, just seconds after it went down, Jack did too.

The whole scene transformed after that. Agents in black pants and tactical jackets swarmed the street, holding civilians at bay while a select few approached Supergirl. Lena could barely see past the barricades that were quickly being set up. Jack was still down when Lena spotted him from a closer vantage point a minute later. She tried to reach him, but soon felt the hands of another agent at her shoulders, telling her she needed to clear the scene.

“I need to see him,” she tried in vain.

She felt like she might collapse. Her whole body was spent. She looked down at the tablet in her hands and saw a single drop of liquid pool atop the screen. In its reflection, she was crying—not puffy eyed, blotchy skin crying, but cold and emotionless.

Someone on the other side of the barrier started to call her name, but she barely registered it, because Jack was still down. Hadn’t even moved.

“Lena?” the voice called again.

 

She was sitting in an uncomfortable plastic chair. Design over function. Looked more like an art piece than a place to sit your butt on.

Downtown Metropolis. Eleventh floor. The woman at the front desk had stepped away for lunch, but the sign had said to take a seat and that someone would be back shortly.

The voice called her name again and she looked up to see Dr. Milton standing at the end of the hall. She wore long slacks and a flowery blouse. No lab coat. Never a lab coat.

“Are you waiting for Jack?” she asked.

Lena felt herself nod.

Dr. Milton smiled and Lena rose to her feet.

“He’s in room five,” she said. “He’s almost done for the day. Just waiting back on some basic bloodwork.”

“Can I see him?” Lena asked.

“Of course,” Dr. Milton replied, before smiling once more and disappearing down the next hall.

Room five had a blue-green colour palette. A white noise machine hummed from the corner and the window at the far end faced the building next door (one of the congressional buildings, if Lena remembered correctly). She’d never been in room five before. Definitely preferred room three with its muted creams and whites and the view of the courtyard below.

“You found the place alright?” Jack spoke up from his seat next to the exam table.

He was failing to hide a rather cheeky grin. Lena shook her head, also failing to hide a smile.

“I have been here before,” she replied, before leaning over to place a kiss on his cheek.

He shrugged. “I almost forgot how to get here, myself.”

“Well let’s hope you forget again,” she’d replied.

 

The van was turning into a garage below a large building now. It looked like a manufacturing facility, lest the many armed agents posted at every door.

“Where are we?” Lena asked.

“I thought you might already know,” the woman replied without missing a beat.

Lena clicked her tongue but said nothing in return.

When they reached what looked like the bottom of the garage, the van came to a halt, and the two guards up front stepped out and shut their doors.

Lena moved to undo her seatbelt, when the woman said, “Thank you for protecting her secret.”

The woman’s eyes were softer when Lena spun to face her. She looked more like the woman from the bar than some grey-site government agent.

Alex. The sister.

Lena replied, “you’re welcome.”

Then they sat there in silence for a beat.

“That man,” Alex began. “He’s your friend?”

“He…” Lena trailed off, feeling hollow and lost again. “Yes, he was.”

“But not now?”

“He’s also my ex,” she supplied, as if it sufficiently answered that and any follow-up questions.

Alex huffed out a muted laugh.

“Yeah. Okay. No explanation necessary there.”

 

After leaving the van, Alex escorted Lena into an elevator before descending another three floors. She would definitely have no service down here (if she had her phone) and wondered how the government employees coped.

“Officially, you aren’t here,” Alex said as they stepped off. “Is that clear?

“I don’t even know where here is.”

“Right answer.”

The corridor was long and when they reached the end, Alex glanced down at a tablet in her hands, before directing Lena into the last room on the right. The door was heavy. Alex had to step in and grasp the top. Finally, with a strong whoosh, it swung out and they were met with a barrage of voices and beeping.

In the centre of the barren room was a single hospital bed. Jack was lying still on top of it as other agents in similar garb paced around him, hooking up wires and adjusting a tube apparatus that ran down his throat.

“Jack,” Lena couldn’t help but breathe out.

A woman with short hair beckoned for Alex and she moved away to speak with her, leaving Lena motionless in the doorway. Eventually the sensor for Jack’s pulse was brought online and that—the repetitive beep of Jack’s heart—finally brought Lena back down to Earth.

“Do you have any idea what’s in his system?” Alex asked after returning to her side.

“Nano-cells,” Lena replied bluntly, as if this was everyday tech on the market.

Alex didn’t blink, but she continued on, explaining her and Jack’s device and its moderate success in cancer treatment and surveillance.

“When was he diagnosed?” Alex asked next.

Lena huffed out a sour laugh.

“Early twenties. The first time. I thought he was better,” she finished bitterly.

Alex nodded and returned her gaze to the tablet in her hand. Data was quickly loading on screen. Everything from diastolic pressure to blood-oxygen levels.

“These numbers don’t make sense,” Alex concluded. “How…” She went quiet when Lena turned away, unable to meet her gaze.

“I couldn’t let him keep going,” Lena finally said. She sighed, gazing down at her heels which were now scuffed beyond recognition. “The other woman—”

“—Beth Breen. We intercepted her on the scene. She’s in holding now.”

“Yes, her. The tablet she had was controlling him, but when I went to override the program, it wouldn’t reconnect. I had to shut it down. I had to.”

Lena could feel her eyes going glossy again, but she would not cry here in front of all these people. She wouldn’t let herself. She tried to channel all the frustration and anger she’d once possessed for her long-time friend, but knowing he hadn’t been himself for months, maybe even years, made that task nearly impossible.

“From our readings,” Alex began again, cautiously, “it appears that the nano-cells in his body have become too integrated to separate from his host cells. Without them functioning, it’s not just his higher functioning that’s failing, but his vital organs.”

Alex took a deep breath. Lena could feel the woman’s eyes on her, but refused to look up.

“Without that program running again, he won’t last long.”

Lena nodded. Her fingers gripped at the edge of her sleeves as she tried to settle herself, but it was a losing battle. Somehow, she’d known the moment he went down. Still, to hear the news out loud made her whole body go numb.

“Is there any way you can bring the code back online?” Alex asked as she led Lena by the elbow to the table situated at the far side of the room.

Lena stared down at Beth’s tablet. There was a new crack in the screen that ran through the centre like a bolt of lightning. Lena ran her finger over the sharp edge, before picking the device up. The screen lit up immediately and Lena was faced with Version 2.1 again. This time the inputs in the background had faded and all it said now was disconnected.

Lena turned to Alex, unsure what was about to come out of her mouth, when a figure entered her periphery. Lena fought through her suddenly dry mouth and muttered, “maybe,” but her attention was elsewhere.

There, in the doorway to the medical bay, Kara stood, still in her Supergirl suit. The skin around her ears was covered in soot and there was blood—Jack’s blood—on the suit sleeve. Other than the grimace creating frown lines around her mouth, she looked perfectly fine.

Lena felt the sudden urge to escape, but with Jack unconscious on the bed and Alex hovering next to her, even Lena’s deepest anxieties couldn’t force her feet to move.

“Has everything been taken care of?” Kara said, eyes cast toward her sister.

Alex confirmed, then shot her a pointed look, nodding towards the bed.

“I heard,” Kara said, forlorn. “You said there might be some way to save him, though?”

Oh. Kara was talking to Lena.

“I—uh.”

Why wasn’t her damn voice working?

“Yes. It’s possible,” she finally said.

Kara bit at her bottom lip as she nodded. Lena hadn’t noticed at first, but it too was outlined in a sheen of grime, probably from all the asphalt dust that had been kicked up during the fight.

Lena tore her gaze away and coughed.

“Water?” Alex asked from beside her.

She accepted a small paper cup gratefully and took a swig. When she looked back in search of a trash bin, Alex looked a little pleased.

“So, the uh. This program?” Kara asked.

Lena breathed a sigh and took over. “Yes. I don’t know if I can recover the old one that was connected to the nano-cells, but I think I may be able to wipe the system and install a new one.”

“And what do you need to do that?” Alex asked.

“My old code would be fastest. It’s not perfect, but it’s better than this,” Lena said dropping the tablet back to the table, “or no program at all.”

 

“So where is the code now?” Alex asked.

They were across the hall in an empty conference room, free from the constant beeping of the medical bay.

“My old laptop. It’s back at my apartment. I can go there now.”

“No,” Alex said, standing quickly. “There’s not enough time.”

She looked over to where Kara stood, pacing in front of the table.

“Supergirl can help you.”

“I can?”

“She can?” Lena and Kara said at the same time.

They both ducked away after making brief eye-contact and focused intently on Alex.

“You can fly there,” Alex said.

Immediately, Lena’s cheeks heated, imagining Kara lifting her into her arms and flying them across the city. Lena didn’t think she could last the journey before either passing out or dying from embarrassment. Still…

“You want me to fly her there and back?” Kara asked.

Lena gulped. When she chanced a glance back at Kara, it looked like her cheeks had pinked as well.

“No,” Alex said, guffawing. “That won’t be necessary.”

Lena sank back in her chair, visibly deflating.

“I’ll give Lena one of the extra comms and she can direct you to where the laptop is located.”

Kara agreed, but her brows were pinched together.

“And how is she going to get it? Break a window? I would really like to get my security deposit back,” Lena said.

“Oh, don’t worry,” Alex said, shaking her head with a smile. “We’ve discussed the window breaking issue many times before.”

Kara shot her sister a withering glare, but after a beat, seemed to accept it.

“We also found this in the coffee shop after,” Alex said. She picked Lena’s purse off the chair beside her and handed it across the table. “I believe your cell phone is in there as well.”

“Thank you,” Lena replied. “And thank you for, you know.”

She motioned toward Kara.

For a split-second, the weight of the situation seemed to slip away like a sheet, covering the elephant in the room. Kara accepted the thanks with a bashful smile. Alex just chuckled silently to herself.

“Well, not like this isn’t fun, but we should probably—” Alex began

“Right. Of course.”

Lena dug her hand into her purse and soon found the outline of her keys.

“The one next to the donut keychain gets you into the building and the silver one is for my front door. Apartment 413.”

When she reached out to hand the keys to Kara, their fingers brushed. Lena fought down the sudden need to babble and returned her hand to her chin.

“Cute,” Kara replied, running her fingers over the plastic figurine. “You really do have all the toys.”

Lena beamed, unable to hold back a smile, when Alex cleared her throat.

“Come on. Save this disaster for another time. We’re still in the middle of today’s.”

 

Before long, Kara returned with Lena’s laptop and its power cord.

“I see they were able to get you a change of clothes,” Kara said, while Lena fussed with the cord, untangling it until it was long enough to plug into the wall.

”Oh, yeah,” Lena replied, glancing down at her government issued track pants. They were a little too long so Lena had rolled up the ends. “I can’t believe I’ve gone my whole life without them.”

Kara chuckled lightly at the comment; however, there still remained this impenetrable barrier between them, so inconspicuous someone might miss it.

After almost a full minute of nothing but intermittent beeping, Lena’s laptop finally started to load the operating system.

Lena cleared her throat, solely intending to fill the empty silence, when Kara commented, “You know, I’m not exactly busy right now. I could fly back to your place and pick up a change of clothes. I don’t know why I didn’t think of that earlier.” Her face screwed up into this little pout that seemed at odds with her bold Supergirl suit. 

To Lena, a pair of her own pants sounded heavenly right now—also some not-damp underwear, but there was no way in hell she was going to direct Supergirl through the drawers in her closet over a comm. A comm other agents at this facility definitely had access to. No. Definitely not.

”You don’t have to. I promise I’m good. Perfect.” Lena tried to appear content, but she knew her eyes were betraying her.

Kara didn’t push, thankfully, and just nodded, before pointing at the door.

”I should leave you to it. Looks like you’re computer is good to go.”

Lena glanced down to the log-in screen and nodded.

”I’ll, uh, be around,” she added, before backing out of the room and hitting her shoulder on the doorframe in the process.

She left Lena promptly after that so Lena could get to work. Wiping the old program installed on the tablet was no trouble once she got started, but soon ran into her first hurdle.

Her AI program hadn’t just had a few bugs in it before. It hadn’t been complete. Without proper testing, Lena had no idea what would happen if she reconnected Jack too early. By Alex’s latest calculations, Jack had six hours before his kidneys—the organs comprised of the largest percentage of nano-cells—would begin to fail. After that, it wouldn’t be long before others would follow suit.

She had less than six hours to fix a code she couldn’t debug over the course of six months. No biggie.

 

After two hours, she’d made no headway with the biggest bug that had caused the program to fail thirty percent of the time but had adapted it for use on the tablet. Before she could progress, she needed to make sure the new program could connect to the nano-cells in Jack’s body.

Lena left the conference room and strayed into the hall, looking for Alex or any of the other agents. Instead, a man dressed in jeans and a Third Eye Blind T-shirt rounded the corner, sipping from the biggest mug of coffee Lena has ever seen.

When he saw Lena, he pivoted to walk away, but not before she was able to call after in.

“Hey!” she shouted.

He was too slow, trying to stop his sacred coffee from overflowing the corners of the mug, and she easily caught up to him.

“I’m looking for Alex Danvers,” she explained in a rush of air. “It’s urgent.”

The man nodded and lowered the mug to his side.

“I would call her, but I have no signal in here.”

Lena chuckled morosely. “I know. Ridiculous, right?”

He nodded in agreement, but his eyes were a tad too shifty for Lena’s liking.

“Okay… I’m going to see if I can find someone else.”

She shot him one last look, before heading back down the hall. There were two agents stationed in Jack’s medical bay. One turned and looked up at Lena when she entered, while the other continued to type away at a screen near his bed.

“I need to test the program to see if it will connect. Are you able to help me with that?”

The agent glanced down at Beth’s tablet in Lena’s hand, then over to Jack.

“I’m probably not the best person to ask when it comes to techy stuff.” He paused, pulling off his gloves, before approaching. “Did you happen to catch Winn. He’s way better with that stuff.”

Winn, huh?

“What’s Winn better at?” Alex asked, barreling into the medical bay.

The other agent stepped back, before handing Alex one of their own tablets.

“I need to make sure the new program will actually connect,” Lena explained as Alex went over Jack’s vitals.

It was just past six o’clock in the morning. There were no windows on this sub-level floor. On a regular morning, Lena would be settling into bed, ready to let sleep take her after another night shift at Catco.

Lena moved to Jack’s bedside, coming within a few feet for the first time since they’d sat across from each other at the café earlier that morning. She imagined reaching out and laying a hand over the arm placed delicately atop the white sheet covering most of his body, but she didn’t know if that would leave her with any less tension than she already felt.

Years ago, Jack’s hugs were like a balm to her constant worries. Now, imagining his hands over her skin was akin to a burn.

“It should be fine to connect if just for a few seconds,” Alex said, moving to Jack’s other side.  

As she replaced the IV drip next to his bed, Lena unlocked the tablet and held it tight in front of her.

“When can I—”

“Give me a second,” Alex said. “I want to make some adjustments first.”

Lena bit at the edge of her lip. She tasted salty sweat in the corner and tried swallowing the unpleasant  taste down, but it didn’t dissipate.

“I think you’re the dumbest smart person I’ve ever met,” Alex said, just loud enough for Lena to hear.

She didn’t meet Lena’s gaze, but the corners of her lips perked up a little, like she was trying not to show too much enjoyment from Lena’s no doubt, shocked expression. Lena harumphed and tapped the edge of the tablet impatiently.

She would not take the bait.

“How’s that?”

Alex’s smarmy smile grew.

“Like I said. Dumbest smart person.”

Lena scowled and paced the room, before returning to Jack’s bedside.

“Are you done?”

“Almost,” Alex replied.

She watched the monitor closely, and when she seemed satisfied with his sinus rhythm, she turned her focus to Lena.

“I won’t pretend to understand everything that goes through that genius brain of yours, but as the concerned and put-upon sister, answer me one question.”

Lena cocked her head to the side, waiting.

“Have you always felt like you needed to solve everything on your own?”

It wasn’t the question Lena had been expecting. She’d essentially been bracing herself for the older sister gauntlet. Everything from what are your intentions with my sister to how do you expect to treat her right when you’ve lied to her for the last two months?

Lena’s mouth hung open and she sputtered.

“No,” she finally replied.

Alex rolled her eyes but didn’t push.

“Okay. He’s ready. Go ahead and try to connect.”

 

The funny thing about being on a ticking timer, not just for a project for school or work, but to save a friend’s life, was that Lena felt no less in her element than any other day in front of the screen. There was a constant supply of hot coffee—provided by the skittish Winn. Every minute not typing went faster than when her fingers were flying across the keys. And at the end of it, she was never satisfied.

Lena scooted back from her chair in the conference room and growled.

The code was decent, but decent wasn’t good enough with Jack’s life on the line. Decent code wouldn’t help her sleep that night or any other night for the foreseeable future.

She had just over an hour before Jack would reach a critical point. Any delay after that could permanently damage his internal organs or even his brain. Lena rolled her shoulders and pulled herself back to the table with a new wave of determination.

She thought back to the day she had sat at her kitchen counter, coding her Superfriends flagging program. If only this program could be that simple. With the flagging program, the goal was fixed: identify any messages related to Supergirl activities. Human bodies, on the other hand, were so complicated, though.

Lena had taken some biochemistry course electives for fun during university, but she was by no means an expert. What she needed was a biologist who had access to all of Jack’s medical information. Then maybe, just maybe, she’d have a kicking off point to start the nano-cells with.

 

“Winn,” Lena called out, the next time he popped his head into the room to check on her.

“Yeah?” he said, rubbing the back of his neck.

“One of the agents said you’d be the person to ask about tech stuff. Is this accurate?”

Winn laughed nervously. “I mean. I’m okay.”

How disappointing.

“Fine. Now, let’s say you had access to what I am guessing is a very robust, and officially non-existent, government system.” Lena tipped her screen down so he couldn’t avoid her gaze and steepled her hands in front of her. “In this scenario, would you be able to ascertain a number of medical records for one individual?”

Winn nodded slowly, before he realized he was even doing so.

“In this scenario, I suppose that would be possible.”

“Perfect.”

Lena rose and snapped her laptop shut. She tucked into under her arm and stalked out of the room. When Winn didn’t immediately follow, she turned back and studied him.

“Are you coming?”

Winn hesitated in the doorway.

“I thought this was only a scenario?”

“It is. And now we are going to carry it out.”

When he still didn’t make a move, Lena added, “I know how well you follow internet regulations. Now come along.”

 

Events passed in quick succession once they were able to pull all of Jack’s electronic medical files from his earlier oncology visits. With Alex by her side, Lena made quick work of the rest of the code. Now all they could do was wait as the program connected and the nano-cells came back online.

Kara stood at Lena’s side. They were at least a foot apart, but her presence still comforted Lena in ways she hadn’t expected.

“His oxygen levels are high enough that I should be able to take him off the ventilator now,” Alex said.

Lena acknowledged her with a slight tilt of her head, as Alex started preparing her supplies at Jack’s bedside.

“How’s it going?” Winn asked from the door to the medical bay.

Both Lena and Kara turned at his entrance, but it was Alex who spoke.

“So far so good.”

She smiled at Lena, but the encouragement was futile against all of Lena’s doubts.

“Was there something you needed?” Alex asked.

Winn stepped all the way into the room, letting the door close. It left only the five of them, Jack’s company included.

“I had a message for Supergirl, actually.”

Kara turned with brows raised. Winn’s eyes flitted around the room at all of them before he just came out with it.

“Ja—your friend said to give you a heads up that your, you know, will be arriving early this morning.”

“What? Who?” Kara asked.

“You know…” Winn side-eyed Lena. “Meow.”

“Oh, for Heaven’s sake!” Alex groaned, halfway through removing Jack’s tubes. “She’s read all your company messages, Winn. I think it’s okay to just say Cat!”

No one said a thing until Alex turned back to her work. Finally, Kara sent Lena a tentative smile, before fleeing from the room using a little of her super speed in the process.

 

When Lena woke later that morning, it took her a moment to recall the events of the last ten hours. Even still, she didn’t recognize the office or the couch she was now lying on. An analog clock hanging from the opposite wall told her it was almost one o’clock in the afternoon.

Slowly, she sat up, rubbing fiercely at her eyes when they wouldn’t focus properly for her. The office was the same shade of grey that the rest of the government facility was, so she must not have been moved far. Speaking of which… how did she get here?

As if on cue, the door to the office opened and Winn peeked in. He was wearing slacks now and a checkered button-down shirt. A Catco employee badge swung from his belt loop.

“You’re awake,” he said with a smile.

“How astute,” Lena replied, around a yawn.

Winn pouted, before moving fully into the office and taking a seat against the desk.

“Where am I?” she asked, once fully upright.

“Alex’s office.”

Lena scanned the items on the desk, now able to make out a placard that said Assistant Director.

“And how did I get here exactly? The last thing I remember is waiting by Jack’s bed in one of your terribly uncomfortable chairs.”

“They’re not my chairs,” Winn rebutted, before sighing. “You fell asleep sometime after eight a.m., so Super—Kara moved you in here.”

Lena nodded, but her brain was screaming in protest. How unfair was it that the first time those strong arms had been around her, Lena was out cold? What if this wouldn’t just be the first time, but the only time?

Lena blinked, forcing the thoughts to the back of her mind as Winn began to talk again.

“Alex wanted to send you home,” Winn continued, “but Kara argued that it was better to have you close by in case they needed your help again.”

That made sense. Still, she was a little bitter they hadn’t thought to consult her on this. Lena played with the ends of her shirt.

“And where is—” she now?

“Jack’s still in the medbay, but his vitals have improved. Last time I checked he hadn’t woken up, though.”

“Oh, right. That’s...”

Lena avoided his gaze, searching the floor around the couch for her heels.

I guess Supergirl took those off while I was asleep as well.

“Would you like to see him?” Winn asked.

 

He didn’t look much different than when Lena had fell asleep at his bedside earlier. Despite this, one of the agents assigned to the room assured Lena that the nano-cells were functioning appropriately. The steady breaths leaving his nose at least told Lena that his lungs—the place the cancer had first been spotted—were working sufficiently.

“He’ll probably always need the nano-cells, but Alex is convinced she’ll be able to wain him off some of them with time,” Winn said.

Lena had assumed as much.

Winn stepped out for a minute, leaving them alone. Lena pulled up a chair until the front pressed against the plastic bedrails.

“We make quite the pair, you stupid, stupid man.” Lena sighed, affectionately.

She reached out and placed her hand over his chest, watching as it rose and fell in a steady rhythm. Most people said their loved ones looked small and weak in hospital beds, hooked up to wires. Lena supposed this was true for many, but when she let her eyes travel down the man in front of her, all she saw was stubbornness and strength.

They were truly two peas in one bull-headed pod.

 

Winn returned shortly with another agent to take vitals on the hour. He saw her sitting close with her hand over the Jack’s chest and smiled, but it was guarded and wan.

“Kara’s at work until five, but she should be back later if you want to come back.”

Lena shook her head faintly.

“No, that’s okay. I should probably get some proper sleep and for that matter, text my boss that I won’t be in tonight.”

“I understand,” Winn replied. He shuffled on the spot, before taking a deep breath. “I think I owe you a huge apology.”

Lena raised her hand to stop him.

“Don’t,” she said. Her words were firm but lacked any bite. “Text me if anything changes with him and I’ll consider us even.”

She went to tear a sheet off one of the spare medical pads, when Winn’s hand stopped her movements.

“No,” he said, a resolute arch to his brow. “I don’t think we will be for a long time. But regardless, thank you.”

He let her go then so she could write her number down. When she looked back up, holding the scrap of paper out, the intensity had fled from his eyes, leaving that perpetual hint of levity behind.

“Take care, Lena,” he said.

She said, “Thanks,” before muttering under her breath, “I’ll try.”

18. Kara

Summary for the Chapter:

In the aftermath of that night, Kara and Lena finally work on moving forward.

Despite being passed up on the story covering Supergirl’s latest fight, Kara still appreciated the opportunity to assist with ‘research’ on Jack’s backstory and motives. Something about the whole situation just screamed Alex. Though not even Alex’s intimidating demeaner nor fancy government badge could apparently persuade Snapper into handing over that juicy of a story to a junior reporter.

Just after 10 a.m., Kara got up to stretch her legs and found herself in the breakroom closest to Winn’s desk.

“So, what do people actually think happened that morning?” he asked upon seeing Kara step forward to refill her water bottle.

“Winn,” Kara chastised, as she glanced around them.

Thankfully, the only other person in the room was at the far side by the windows, enraptured in their own phone call.

“Kara,” Winn teased.

She shot him an aggrieved look until he backed off.

“Fine, fine. I guess I’ll just wait until the story is released tomorrow morning. Speaking of, where is your favourite IT person this morning.”

“How are those topics at all related?”

Winn shrugged, smirking over the rim of his mug. He took a sip, never taking his eyes of Kara.

“I don’t know,” she replied, trying to come off as unaffected.

“I see,” Winn replied. “Well, you could just text her. Or email if that’s more your speed.”

Kara shook her head.

“I don’t need to do either, Winn. She probably doesn’t want to hear from me after everything that happened. Plus, Alex told me Jack was released from the DEO this morning. They need time to catch up.”

Winn eyed her sceptically. “While I agree with you on the latter point, why on Earth would she not want to hear from you, Kara? After everything that happened, I would think she’d care more now than ever to talk with you. You can’t just pretend like the last thirty-six hours were normal.”

Kara rubbed at her eyes. How could she explain to him that people who want to talk don’t ghost each other?

“She needs time, and I can give her that, okay?”

Winn sighed dramatically. “I understand why Alex wants to pull her hair out sometimes. Both of you are exasperating.”

“Look, if she—”

Kara paused as a co-worker walked past them, headed for the fridge.

At a lower volume, Kara finished, “—if she was interested, she could have called.”

“That’s funny,” Winn replied. “I could’ve sworn you had the ability to call her too. Not to mention, Lena seemed more interested in your whereabouts when she woke up on Alex’s couch than that tall British dream.”

“I need to get back to work,” Kara seethed, before shooting Winn another glare and pacing away conspicuously fast.

 

There was another assignment waiting on her desk when Kara got back. She could feel Snapper’s gaze on her back and sat down immediately to go over the notes he’d left her. Once he’d gone though, Kara’s eyes were back on her phone which sat annoyingly quiet on her desk next to her computer.

If she wanted to see me, she’d have come back last night or at least texted something, Kara thought to herself.

She was so lost in thought, going over the short conversations between her and Lena the previous morning, that she didn’t notice there was an incoming call on her desk phone until it flashed red for a new voicemail.

Kara groaned, before raising the receiver to her ear and hitting Messages.

“Hello Ms. Danvers. This is Dan from the front desk. There’s a delivery down here for you that needs to be signed for.”

Delivery?

Kara raised her finger to call back, wondering if this was some BS from Cat, but reconsidered.

 

A minute later she was coming off the elevator on the ground floor, headed for the front desk. Dan, an older gentleman who’d been working for Catco since Kara had started, met her eyes across the lobby and smiled.

“Oh, I’m glad you got my message,” he said.

Kara smiled back, before her attention moved on to the teenager leaning against the other side of the counter. He had a cooler hanging from his arm with the logo of one of the food delivery services plastered across the front.

Kara couldn’t help but perk up when she thought of the Wrapzone deliveries Lena had left for her in the past.

“I’m Kara,” she told the guy, motioning for his phone so she could sign the screen as neatly as possible. Her signature looked like messy child scribbles, but the delivery guy didn’t bat an eye.

“Thanks,” he replied quietly, before sliding a plastic bag over.

It was filled to the brim with containers. Clearly not Wrapzone. Huh. Kara couldn’t help but feel a little let down.

“Smells very good,” Dan said once the delivery guy had left. “My wife loves their Orange chicken.”

A faded receipt was taped to the other side. Kara plucked it off the bag and read down the order.

Pork Potstickers x1

Ginger and Chick’n Potstickers x1

Soup Dumplings x1

Veg Potstickers x1

“Do let me know how you like it,” Dan continued.

Kara opened her mouth to ask if this was his doing but realized quickly there was only one person that could have sent this.

“I will,” Kara replied, before lifting the bag off the counter.

She turned, trying to decide if she wanted to head back up to her floor to eat in the breakroom or find some tables outside, when the unique scent of Cat’s perfume wafted across the lobby. Kara’s head jerked up in time to see her former boss strutting towards the door with her sunglasses already lowered in place.

“Lunch?” Cat asked when she was only a few feet from Kara.

Kara floundered, wondering if four full-sized orders of potstickers and dumplings were too much for a single human. Cat didn’t give her a chance to respond. She shot Kara a sly smile, before picking up her pace and pushing open the front doors.

 

Deciding she was still frustrated with Winn, Kara found a table in the courtyard behind Catco so she could plow through her lunch alone. She wanted to pretend she wasn’t the type of person to cave easily, but after trying the Pork Potstickers, she absolutely had to ask Lena where she’d ordered them from. They were the perfect size, packed full of ingredients and just firm enough to have a satisfying chew. Plus, the sauce. Kara could drink a whole glass of it.

[Kara] I’m assuming you were responsible for my lunch delivery today.

[Kara] If so, thank you. It was delicious.

She tapped the table beside her empty containers and wondered if Lena would be awake yet. After the longest minute known to man, her screen lit up.

[Lena] You are welcome.

[Lena] I’m glad you enjoyed it.

Kara typed back quickly, letting her thoughts take her without thinking too hard about what she was doing.

[Kara] I don’t recognize the packaging. Where’s the food from?

Those three pesky dots appeared almost instantly, before disappearing and appearing again. Finally, she had a response.

[Lena] Why don’t I show you tomorrow night?

Well then.

That was an invitation, right?

Kara bit at her lip, hiding a smile from the slowly emptying courtyard around her.

[Kara] I would like that.

[Kara] It will be nice to hang out under more relaxed circumstances.

She smiled down at her phone, running the conversation over again in her brain, if only to leave her in a better mood than when she’d come down here earlier.

However, the second time through, she stopped.

 

“So? How does it read?”

Kara peered over Winn’s shoulder as he reread Kara’s exchange with Lena.

“Well,” Winn drew out, foot tapping against the carpet under his desk, incessantly.

She couldn’t see his face from her angle but had a feeling that weighted well was not positive.

“It sounds like you’re excited to hang out.”

Kara hung her head, before holding out her hand for her phone.

“Rao, have I really just fucked everything up?”

Winn harumphed, possibly from her choice words.

“No, probably not.”

He didn’t sound particularly convincing. Kara let out a whimpering groan. 

“I’m sure if you finish off your platonic hang out dinner with some light making out, it will clear everything right up.”

Kara smacked Winn in the shoulder and glared. Silent treatment back on, she decided.

 

At the end of work, Kara still had no idea how she was going to slyly change her and Lena’s ‘hang out’ dinner to a date. Around 4:30, she’d even considered Winn’s suggestion, before realizing how potentially scarring that could be, and reconsidered.

Maybe she could suggest picking Lena up. That was sort of date-like. In the elevator down, Kara started to type out a message to Lena when she paused. She didn’t actually have a car, so would stopping by Lena’s apartment first still be considered ‘picking her up’?

Kara moved over to her conversation with Alex when the elevator doors opened. She flicked her gaze up briefly and had to double-take. Lena was entering from one of the lower floors holding a packet of paper. Her face was stone cold, and she was wearing a pair of jeans and a light sweater instead of the professional get-up she’d been in thirty-six hours before. She was even wearing a pair of white sneakers instead of boots or heels.

“Lena,” Kara choked out.

She tucked her phone into her back pocket and looked up to see a conflicted expression crossing Lena’s features.

“Kara,” Lena finally replied sounding just as surprised.

“What are you doing here?” Kara continued lamely.

The doors closed, and still, Lena’s gaze seemed to settle everywhere except Kara. After a rough swallow that was audible to the whole elevator, Lena straightened.

“Well,” she said, chuckling without much mirth. “I was just let go, so these would be my termination papers.

She held the paper packet up as if it were exhibit A in a courtroom trial.

“You were fired?” Kara yelled, incredulously.

It came out a little loud, but all Kara could think was, how dare they.

After Lena nodded, Kara pressed, “How could they do that?”

Lena’s smile was tight as she tucked the papers under the arm holding her purse.

“Quite easily. See, while it’s not against Catco’s code of conduct, I suppose they prefer their employees not be caught on camera knocking out a woman and stealing her tablet. I’m just surprised they didn’t make a dig at my family during the exit interview.”

Kara gaped. Lena just shrugged, before turning back to the front of the elevator primly.

“Lena…”

“It could have gone a lot worse, honestly. They still gave me a fair severance package since the fight didn’t take place while on Catco property. Shaun—my boss—even sounded sympathetic when I was called in today.”

“I’m sorry,” Kara eventually said. It didn’t feel like enough.

They both stepped out into the lobby when the elevators opened a moment later. As they reached the front doors, Kara reached out and laid her hand over Lena’s covered forearm.

“Hey,” she said, stopping Lena from parting ways with her just yet. “My friends and I have these game nights some weekends and since you’re basically already an honorary superfriend…”

“You want me to come to your friends’ game night?” Lena picked at the edge of her packet of papers, folding over the corner on an industrial sized staple. “I don’t know.”

Kara glanced away from Lena and moved to open the door for them. After mumbling a quiet thanks, Lena took her first few steps toward the door, leaving a fragrant swell of air in her wake. Kara had to stop herself from swaying into it as Lena brushed by. The perfume was somehow exactly what Kara would’ve expected someone like Lena to wear: heady and warm, with undertones of spice. She logged that information away before catching up with her by the sidewalk.

“Think about it?”

Lena seemed reluctant, but eventually nodded.

“Listen, I—”

“Do you—”

They both started at the same time. Lena motioned for Kara to go on.

“Do you need a lift home?” Kara asked.

Lena stared blankly back at her as Kara adjusted the glasses resting on her nose.

“You mean…” Lena trailed off.

“—joking,” Kara added.

For heaven’s sake.

“But I could walk with you,” she offered.

Lena glanced back to the ground, before resolutely nodding her head.

“I would be amiable to that.”

Kara waited for Lena to pick a direction, before they started down the street, matching pace quickly.

When they had made it half a block away from the small crowd congregating outside the business park, Kara looked up and said, “The Superhero jokes. They’re quite bad, but you’ll get used to them eventually.”

Lena looked taken back by the comment, but when the words had settled, she merely smiled and murmured, “I’m sure I will.”

It was odd. After so many weeks of thinking about Lena as both the Shoebox Lady from IT and the striking woman from accounting, walking beside her should have felt anything but normal and mundane. It was comfortable in the way that two might lounge around in the same space and not be inclined to break the silence.

Eventually though, Lena adjusted the packet of papers in her hands and her shoulder brushed Kara’s and it was fine, nice, pleasant. But then Kara wondered how to do that again and maybe not by accident—definitely not by accident—and then the mundaneness of their early evening stroll was anything but.

“Is this weird?” Kara finally asked, breaking the silence.

They’d been walking for almost three blocks without saying a word. In Kara’s world, three minutes of quiet was an anomaly.

“A little, but that’s not on you,” Lena replied, rushing to add the assurance.

“You say that, but I’m pretty sure I’m the super-powered alien from another planet and you’re—”

Brilliant? Breathtaking? Bound to realize associating yourself with me is usually a mistake?

“—you’re…”

“Trying to stop you from constantly blowing your secret identity?” Lena shot her an unimpressed look, and Kara mimed zipping her lips real quick.

“Sorry,” she confessed a moment later.

“Hey, I know what I’ve gotten myself into.”

They both laughed quietly to themselves, before Lena seemed to recall something suddenly and her face soured.

“There is now the issue of me no longer having access to my old Catco-issued laptop. The laptop that not only has plenty of sensitive information about you still on it, but also evidence that I’ve been secretly removing said information and overwriting it on Catco servers.”

Lena pulled at the collar to her sweater and pursed her lips.

“That may have been a detail I overlooked after storming out of that stupid HR office earlier.”

Kara gaped at her, unsure if this hint of humour meant the situation wasn’t that big a deal or she saw no other course of action but to force levity.

“I can message Alex if you want,” Kara replied.

“You think she can help?”

Kara nodded and took out her phone. “She has her ways. I can ask her now.”

Lena seemed more at ease following the suggestion. After she’d steadied her breathing, Kara turned her attention back on Lena.

“See how much easier it is when you don’t have to do everything on your own?”

Lena abruptly halted, forcing Kara to slow to a stop as well.

With her free hand planted firmly on her waist, she said, “Wait, did you and your sister discuss this flaw of mine or is it just universally known?”

She looked completely serious, but Kara was quickly learning Lena’s tells. The slight arch to one of her eyebrows told Kara, for instance, that this overt show of shock was Lena’s attempt at playful banter. The playful aspect was slightly overshadowed by the intensity of her gaze, however Kara could overlook that, given it was also extremely appealing.  

“I’m usually the one that Alex gets upset with for trying to balance everything on her own,” Kara said, biting her bottom lip. “So, I may be an expert on the subject.”

“Is that so? Never would have guessed.”

 

The remaining distance to Lena’s apartment was spotted with short conversations of little importance, yet Kara treasured each. She’d remembered the approximate location of Lena’s place, and purposefully began dragging her feet as the city blocks still to go dwindled into the single digits.  

“So anyway, what’s this I hear about you being some computer genius?”

“I don’t know where you heard that.”

“Like you didn’t just code a human AI interface in less than six hours.”

“It was already partially written…”

“Oh, I’m aware. Partially written by you, though.”

“Kara,” she whined.

“Lena,” Kara countered, as she narrowed her eyes.

“Most of the code took a long time.”

“Sure,” Kara chuckled.

She bumped shoulders with Lena, before falling in line beside her, snickering at Lena’s appalled expression.

 

“So, this is me,” Lena said, when they came to a stop outside the renovated mid-century apartment building.

“It’s nice,” Kara replied, hovering outside the lobby doors. She brought her hand up to her face and blocked the sun from her eyes so she could peer in.

“It’s adequate,” Lena countered. “And you would already know that since you’ve been inside. That is unless there’s a broken window I’m not aware of.”

“No. No broken windows. I was in a hurry before and didn’t stop to take it all in.”

Lena barked out a laugh.

“Well,” she began, unlocking the main door and holding it open for Kara. “This is the atrium. Notice the bland city-scape artwork and customary photos of the beach—required for all generic apartment complexes in coastal cities. Through there is the business centre which features two computers from 2010 and a printer that only connects via a LAN connection. The door at the back leads to the leasing office where a particularly dramatic fellow named Mark occasionally returns my maintenance calls, and that side nook over there—that’s for the mail.”

She ended her tour with a hand flourish, before dropping her packet of paper on a counter nearby and rounding the corner to the mail area.

“That was quite a tour,” Kara said as Lena paced down the narrow corridor, looking for her mailbox.

“I’d invite you up to continue it, but I’m afraid when it comes to seating options, mine are all terrible. I swear, the most uncomfortable chairs and sofa imaginable.”

“Why’d you buy them then?”

“Rite of passage, I suppose. Never really went through the whole ‘first place, IKEA furniture’ phase.”

“Well,” Kara said, adjusting her glasses. “Some of us weren’t so lucky. Mind you, they sold this egg chair that I loved and would’ve kept if Alex hadn’t badgered me about it being horrendously outdated and tacky.”

“Well, I promise you my alternative wasn’t all that glamorous either.” Lena leaned back against the wall of tiny mailboxes and chuckled. “There was this armchair Jack’s friend donated to us. I use that term very loosely, because I’m like ninety-five percent sure his girlfriend wouldn’t let him keep it in their new house, and he just didn’t feel like lugging it down to some donation place.”

“Anyway…” Lena straightened, suddenly remembering why she’d taken them on a detour to the mail room. After fishing for the smallest key on her keychain, she opened her box and pulled out a flyer, some credit card junk mail, and a few plain white envelopes, before closing it shut a tad harder than necessary.

She blinked down at them as if not really processing what she was holding.

Kara knew it wasn’t her place to say anything, but this was becoming awkward again, and there was no way she was going home on this note.

“How is he? Jack?”

Kara watched as Lena’s shoulders stiffened underneath her sweater. After some obvious stalling, she pulled the key out of the lock and turned to face Kara. Her face was strained, as if she were getting a shot in her arm (Kara had remembered how terrible those felt the last time Alex had jabbed her four times following an unfortunate solar flare) but wanted to keep a brave face on.

Finally, she blinked away some of the tension, and said, “He’s okay. I think.”

The first words out of Kara’s mouth were almost, you think? Somehow, by sheer luck maybe, she reconsidered that choice before replying, “I know you were uh, close. Alex filled me in.”

Great comment, Kara.

“I mean, I know you have a history with him, and you can’t just lock that away.”

Lena laughed morosely and shook her head. Her dark hair spilled over her shoulder, sliding slowly down her sweater as some strands caught on the fabric.

“No, I can’t just lock it away. Believe me, I’ve tried. Too many problems, too small boxes.”

Kara walked closer to comfort her but couldn’t decide if that was what she needed. She was so close, but those few feet between them felt impossible to bridge.

“Listen, I’m not going to make you sit through the woes of my past relationship,” Lena continued, slapping her mail down against her leg. She drew in a staggered breath, before forcefully exhaling. “There’re too many lies, too many false hopes. Just too much. I’ll spoil the ending and tell you it wasn’t pretty.”

“Lena…”

Kara shoved aside her reservations and reached out until she found the knuckles of Lena’s hand and skimmed them with her own fingertips. When Lena didn’t pull away, Kara pressed closer until her touch was firm and solid against the back of her hand. 

“Lena,” she repeated, hoping Lena would meet her gaze. “If you’re worried about judgement, I literally have no leg to stand on when it comes to all of that.”

Maybe the fact that she couldn’t say love or relationships back was an indicator in and of itself. This, however, was not something she wanted to air out right now. Their conversation was about Lena, not her own inability to sustain any form of romantic intimacy.

“You’ve had my back more times than I probably know of in the last couple months. Hell, my secret probably wouldn’t be such a secret anymore if it wasn’t for you—”

“—sorry again about not telling you.”

“I just want to be there for you in return. Not out of obligation, but because I just, want to.”

Kara rubbed her thumb over Lena’s fingers one last time, before pulling away. She couldn’t help the restless energy prickling her skin as she swayed back against her heels.

“If what you need is time with Jack or yourself to figure everything out, then I get it.”

“What do you mean?”

“Never mind. It’s just something stupid Winn suggested I bring up. I shouldn’t have. I just wanted you to know that I understand if you need time right now.”

“Kara—”

“And you shouldn’t feel like you have to humour me just because I came to your rescue—scratch that. That sounds terrible.”

“Kara?”

“I didn’t mean like, you couldn’t save yourself. I mean…fuck! You did save all of us.”

“Kara!”

“Yeah?”

“I don’t need time.”

“No?”

“Definitely not. I did ask you to have dinner with me less than five hours ago.”

“That is true.”

Lena leaned into Kara, nudging her shoulder as she smiled directly at her. It was endearing in a way that felt familiar, even though this was probably the longest conversation they’d had since the bar.

“I know what you said earlier and that we have dinner plans tomorrow, but I was wondering if you’d also like to join me for one of those Superfriends game night this weekend?”

Kara smiled, before rambling on, “I think Alex is bringing her new girlfriend this time too, so we’ll have even numbers again.”

She waited patiently for Lena’s answer, trying not to appear too eager.

“You were serious about that? Crashing your game night with your friends?” Lena clarified.

“Well, it’s not my game night, but yes. I would really like you to come to our Superfriends night.”

Kara forced another smile, but Lena still looked uncertain.

“It’s not that I don’t want to go; I just don’t want to make things awkward. Like what do I say when I meet your friends? Hey, we haven’t been introduced properly, but I’m the one that’s been reading through all your private messages?”  

Kara shrugged. “I know it’s not the best circumstance, but you’ve already spoken with Winn, and James can be… James sometimes, but he’ll get over it.”

For lack of having no free hands to reach out to, Kara rested her own over Lena’s shoulders, and rubbed her thumbs into the grooves of her collar bone, just as Alex had done plenty of times to soothe Kara after a long day. She hoped it had that effect on Lena, because if anything, it was doing the exact opposite to herself. Her whole body felt overheated, like she was floating above a hundred red suns.

“Please come,” Kara said when she’d found it within herself to look Lena in the eye.

Whether Lena realized it or not, her head started to bob up and down in agreement, and then her mouth caught up and she murmured, “okay.”

“Great,” Kara buzzed, barely able to hold back the inclination to pull Lena into a tight hug. “And by the way, Alex’s girlfriend doesn’t know I’m Supergirl,” Kara whispered, “so you’ll have to keep any sensitive information on the downlow.”

“Then I promise to preserve your cover as Lena: regular, unassuming friend.”

She laughed lightly and yes, now that Kara was up close, she could confirm with one-hundred percent certainty, the presence of Lena’s dimples. 

“Actually,” Kara said, feeling her face warm. Here went nothing. “I was thinking you could go as Lena: my date.”

In the echoing atrium of Lena’s building, Kara felt the following seconds pass as if she were suspended in perpetual, empty space. The sound of Lena shuffling in place broke the effect, forcing Kara to lift her gaze.

“That would also be acceptable. More than acceptable, actually.”

Notes for the Chapter:

One chapter to go! I'm going to miss this AU, but will be happy to finally wrap it up.

Please let me know if I've fumbled something! This is edited solely by me, and I am blind to my own mistakes.

19. Lena

Summary for the Chapter:

Epilogue

Notes for the Chapter:

The end is finally here! *throws confetti*

Thank you to those of you that have stuck with this story for so long. It feels like the end of an era for me, but I am very, VERY happy to move onto my next project. I thought for so long that I'd never see the completed check mark next to 'Attachments'. Thanks to so many of your motivating comments over the years, I finally found an ending I was satisfied with. I hope you are happy with it too.

Although Lena had gotten the rundown on Catco’s feature article the night before, Lena still wasn’t prepared to see a picture of Jack fighting Supergirl across the site’s homepage the following morning. Lena sat cross-legged in bed, swiping her way down the webpage until she found Kara’s name buried at the bottom in tiny, slanted letters. Even so, Lena felt a smile creep up her face. This was her Kara. Well not, hers, necessarily. Not yet. Lena shook her head and buried her face into the covers. If only old Lena—the Lena that had just moved cities and knew absolutely no one and had next to no hope for the future—could see her now.

True to their word, Catco had painted Jack in a much better light than Lena had originally feared. Though Beth’s name was completely omitted, an unknown villain had still been blamed for Jack’s behaviour in a way that Lena could only describe as spit-take worthy.

“Oh, Jack,” Lena murmured to herself.

The author of the article had made Jack out to be this helpless scientist in the wrong place at the wrong time, only to be saved by National City’s Supergirl. Kara had seemed so guilty on the phone last night, explaining that she’d not wanted to take all the credit, but Alex had insisted that it was the safest option.

“It’s fine, Kara,” she’d insisted, lounging on her couch just past eight p.m.

“But if people knew, then maybe you’d be able to get your job back, and we’d get to work together again.”

Lena chuckled into her phone. “Kara,” she said, enjoying the fondness that kept coming over her every time she got to say the woman’s name. “Even if I got my job back, I’d never see you while working the night shift.”

She was met with silence and had to pull the phone away from her face to make sure they hadn’t been disconnected.

“Plus, our schedules would constantly conflict, and I wouldn’t be okay with that.”

That. The reminder that there was more between them than work and weighted conversations was enough to get Kara talking again.

“Are we still on for tomorrow night?” Kara asked, shyly.

“Yes,” Lena breathed out, wispy and soft.

She wondered if Kara could hear her heart beating faster from wherever she was taking the call. At home? Still at work putting the final touches on tomorrow morning’s issue? Hovering over the city in her tight suit and ruby red cape?

“Well, I can’t wait.”

Lena smiled and dug her heels into the shag carpet under her toes.

“Listen,” Kara continued, “I’d love to keep chatting, but I better get back.”

“Got a city to save or something?” Lena joked.

“More like a family-sized bucket of chicken fingers to take down, but it’s all in a day’s work.”

Lena barely resisted a loud and unladylike snort, before wishing Kara good luck with that endeavor and saying goodbye.

 

Now Lena stood outside the door to Jack’s hotel room, staring down the fancy gold number plate that read 714 and wishing she knew what to say. She was afforded no more time for thinking as the door swung open and there, Jack stood.

He looked shorter than the last time she’d seen him upright, subtly threatening her in a late-night coffee shop.

“Lena, hey,” he greeted.

His beard was gone, and his hair looked unruly as if he’d just gotten out of bed, despite it already pushing 11 a.m. He stepped away from where he was leaning against the door handle and beckoned her into the room.

It was spacious with a sitting area across from a large floor to ceiling window and a wet bar tucked in between two solid wood doors. Lena passed the second which was cracked open and caught a glimpse of an unmade king-sized bed next to a side table covered in medical supplies and pill bottles. Lena quickly averted her gaze and pretended to admire the view behind Jack when he turned to direct them over to the armchairs.

After taking a seat across from him, she found her gaze falling to his beardless chin, unaccustomed to the appearance.

“It feels as odd as it looks,” Jack said, running a hand over the clean-shaven skin there.

As he raised his chin, Lena finally caught sight of an angry red mark jackknifed across his jawline. Lena couldn’t remember this particular injury. There were a lot of those, though. So many scrapes and torn skin the last time she’d seen him lying almost motionless in the hospital bed at the DEO.

“There was so much dirt and blood,” Jack explained, directing his gaze to his lap where he fiddled with the outline of his phone through his sweatpants pocket. “They didn’t give me much of a choice after slinging around words like infection and abscess.”

They never did.

Lena cleared her throat, forcing her thoughts away from memories that continued to surface more and more recently. Images of Jack waving at her from the oversized chemo-ward armchairs at the end of his weekly appointments.

“It’s a nice place you got here,” Lena commented, trying to break the tension.

Jack looked around, surveying the room.

“Yes, those friends of yours have good taste. They suggested I hang around here a bit until they cleared things up. Didn’t want me to be stopped by some government agents at the airport, eh?”

“So, you’re flying back to Metropolis soon?”

“I hope so. After this mess, I’d kind of like to find some normalcy again. Why? Do I have a reason to stay?”
