He leaned forward, bracing his elbows on his knees as his kind eyes met hers. God, she’d missed them—the way they used to look at her, see her. Until recently, she’d felt as if they’d only seen through her.

In the space between them, Lena reached out and covered his hands with hers. They were so much bigger than hers. Rough and warm, but different than she remembered. She squeezed twice before pulling away and settling back into the stiff cushions at her back.

“The article came out today,” she said.

His face tensed at the mention of it before he seemed to deflate, and his eyes fell away from hers, if only by a fraction.

“I skimmed through it earlier. Though I’m a little bruised in more ways than one, I’m grateful. Please thank your friend.”

Lena’s breath hitched on the last word, and she forced her head to nod up and down.

“They could have torn me to shreds and ruined my life—more than what I’d already done myself.”

“Jack—”

“—don’t, Lena.”

“Okay.”

Lena cleared her hair out of her face, despite its insistence at slipping back almost instantly.

“So, your friend…”

God, did they have to do this?”

“You’ve really grown attached to her.”

Lena had been expecting a question, so when faced with his blunt statement, she did nothing but gape uselessly.

“No matter the circumstances, I will always care about you, Lena,” Jack went on.

“Even after all that’s—”

“—no matter the circumstances.”

Lena nodded; her eyes drew down as she found herself unable to meet his gaze. Just as he went to speak again, there was a knock at his hotel room.

“One second,” he said, before pushing himself up to standing.

There was some shuffling from behind her, then the door to the room opened, and another man’s voice traveled across the space.

“Please come in,” Jack said.

Lena waited until both sets of footsteps crossed the room before turning to see who the new visitor was. The man was dressed in tan pants and a polo shirt today; however, Lena recognized him as one of the scrubbed in nurses from the DEO the morning after the fight.

“Sorry for interrupting,” the nurse said, nodding Lena’s way.

“That’s no problem,” she said, sporting a foreign smile. “We were just finishing up.”

Jack turned her way and frowned but didn’t speak on the contrary.

“I’ll just be setting my stuff up in there,” the nurse said, pointing towards Jack’s bedroom. “So, please, don’t rush.”

While the nurse excused himself, Lena took the time to gather her purse from the floor at her feet. She could feel Jack’s eyes on her but continued to move towards the front door.

“Lena,” he finally called.

She turned, eyes flicking away from the emergency evacuation sign plastered on the back of the door. He was hovering in the hall between what Lena guessed was the bathroom and the door to his bedroom. Though he seemed to use the wall at his side to support himself, the conviction in his voice made him look neither weak nor helpless.

“Does this friend—does she know how amazing you are?”

“She cares about me.”

“Does she know, though?”

Lena opened her mouth to speak and felt her throat close up. It was like the end of a dream when she’d try to say something out loud but find she was unable to make a sound.

“I’ve got to go,” she finally managed.

“Of course,” Jack replied. “I’ve got to get stabbed a few times by my friend.”

The joke didn’t land. Still, Lena forced a breathy laugh, before finally finding the door handle and slipping out.

 

For the remainder of the afternoon and into the early evening, Lena was a nervous mess of a woman. No matter how many times she reminded herself that this was just Kara, her brain would retaliate, internally screaming, just Kara? There is no just Kara.

She’d been living in mostly business casual clothes (Lena’s idea of business casual anyway) and simple outfits for running errands around town, so the prospect of dressing up for a date was beyond stressful. They were going to a Chinese restaurant, so it didn’t exactly call for a cocktail dress, but she still wanted to look nice. It was times like these when she regretted not being one of those women who had a whole list of close friends on speed dial.

By ten to seven, she’d managed to slip into a flattering pair of dark jeans and a sleeveless top that was just low-cut enough to be enticing but not too revealing. She was still barefoot, padding around her kitchen trying to decide if taking a shot of one of her weaker bottles of whisky was too much, when her phone buzzed from the centre island.

[Kara] I’m so sorry, but I need to postpone our dinner.

[Kara] A SG emergency came up. I’ll try to make it over as fast as I can but please don’t wait up for me.

[Kara] I am so sorry Lena.

[Kara] ❤️

Lena stared down at the phone and bit at her lip.

[Lena] Don’t apologize. I understand.

[Lena] Just please be careful.

[Lena] ❤️

She continued to stand in her kitchen in silence for a moment, waiting perhaps for a follow up message. When Kara’s side of the conversation remained inactive for another few minutes, Lena slipped the phone into the back pocket of her jeans and moved to the couch.

Sure enough, the National City news coverage already had footage of Kara arriving on the scene of two shape shifting aliens at the centre of a chaotic intersection. The traffic lights were blinking rapidly between red, yellow, and green, and all around the street cars were stopped and abandoned by their drivers.

Though she knew Supergirl could take them, Lena bit down hard on the inside of her cheek as she watched Kara catch up to the first while the second took off in the opposite direction. The camera barely had time to follow the second before he ran straight at an electrical transformer box and seemed to get sucked into it like a slow-motion morph animation. Footage cut back over to Supergirl after; she was in a heated conversation with the other alien and although it was too blurry to even attempt to lip-read, Lena could tell by the alien’s smirk that the confrontation was far from over.

After two DEO agents had apprehended the first alien, news coverage cut over to one of the broadcasters aimlessly speculating on the cause of the attack. Lena scoffed, before jabbing the power button on her TV remote and moving over to her laptop sitting closed on the coffee table in front of her.

 

The next three hours passed in the blink of an eye. One moment, Lena was reviewing the footage of the aliens standing in the intersection. The next, her phone’s screen was lit up displaying 10:13 p.m. To her dismay, the incoming message was from an unknown number and not Kara’s.

[Unknown] My flight has been rescheduled to Saturday morning. Can we meet up the night before?

[Unknown] This is Jack by the way.

[Unknown] My old Apple account was suspended thanks to Beth.

[Unknown] Guess she broke the terms of service…

[Unknown] Anyway, let me know.

Lena blinked down at the screen, fingers hovering over the reply button.

A new message came in a moment later.

[Unknown] I know you don’t owe me anything, so I would understand if you can’t.

[Unknown] But I do owe you a proper apology and I’d prefer to not do that over the phone.

“Jack,” Lena muttered under her breath.

She’d respond to him later. She was about to lock her phone when another notification came in.

[Kara] You still up?

[Lena] Me? Up? Of course.

[Kara] 🙄

[Lena] Why?

[Kara] Well Alex finally released me from SG duty, and I was thinking I could come by.

[Kara] And don’t worry. If your chairs really are that uncomfortable, I can find somewhere else to sit 😏

Lena blinked down at the phone. Her brows were almost in her hairline as she looked left and right, wondering if she was reading this right. A second later, a follow up came in.

[Kara] 😊

A gust of air rushed past her lips as Lena brought her shaking fingers back to the screen.

[Lena] Yes.

[Lena] You can come by.

She set the phone down on her counter and looked around. The apartment was no dirtier than the morning Kara had retrieved her old laptop for her, but she still felt the impulse to straighten the pile of mail on her coffee table and refold the blanket hanging over her sofa’s armrest.

Her outfit on the other hand…

Lena gave one last cursory scan of her living room, before darting for her bedroom. She was partway there, when she heard the undeniable sound of someone knocking on her front door.

“One sec!” she called.

Of course she had to get herself involved with Supergirl—a woman who could probably cross oceans in minutes. A trip across National City was like a normal human’s jaunt from the couch to the fridge.

Lena pushed the door to her bedroom open and scanned the room. She’d hung her date outfit back up three hours ago; however, she was not answering the door in just sweatpants and a loose tank top.

“Lena?” the voice called from the hall.

“Fuck, fuck, fuck,” Lena hissed under her breath.

She forewent finding a clean bra and settled for the soft, black sweatshirt hung over the chair in the corner of the room. It smelled decent enough, so Lena shrugged it on and hoped the static from the material hadn’t done a number on her hair.

“One sec,” she repeated, tearing into the front hall, thankful her sockless feet prevented her from sliding on the laminate floor.

She flicked open the two locks on her door, and then there she was: Supergirl—Kara.

“Hi,” Lena said.

She could still hear the breathless quality to her voice and honestly couldn’t tell if it was from rushing around for the past minute or the woman standing in front of her now.

“You really did just finish up.”

Kara glanced down at her suit and smiled meekly.

“Yeah,” she admitted, rubbing the back of her neck where her cape disappeared under long blonde hair. “I was worried that if I went home first you might not be awake by the time I came back.”

Lena pursed her lips, before moving away from the door.

“Well, please. Come in.”

It wasn’t until they were both standing in the small foyer that Kara drew her eyes away from Lena’s face and down her body. Kara’s eyes went wide for a moment, before a forlorn expression shadowed any embarrassment that could have been there a moment earlier.

“I am really sorry again about missing our date.”

Lena shoved her hands into the pockets of her sweatpants, and she rocked back on her heels. There was a hole in one of the pockets; she sought it out, quickly finding the loose stitching and winding it around her finger.

“You don’t have to apologize. Ever. I know what dating you entails and—”

“Are we dating?” Kara cut in.

Lena laughed subconsciously. “I was trying to.”

She may not have much experience with relationships and even less with women, but she knew what she wanted.

“Is that okay with you?”

“Oh, definitely,” Kara replied.

Their eyes met across the small space again, before Lena felt soft fingers curling around her palm. Not for the first time, she was pleasantly surprised by just how smooth and warm Kara’s hands were. It wasn’t like she thought they’d be covered in callouses, even though she did spend a good part of her days lifting heavy objects and literally brawling foes in the street. It was the tough and confident exterior that contrasted so starkly with the tenderness of Kara as a person.

After hanging her cape up on one of the hooks by the front door like it was a jacket, Kara followed Lena further into the apartment. It was strange, watching the superhero she’d seen so many times on TV stopping at her overcrowded shoe rack to slide off her own knee-high red boots. Lena blinked at the contrasting sight, before turning away towards her kitchen.

While Lena sought out a meal for them to eat, Kara excused herself to use the bathroom. She’d only ducked into the small powder room for a second before she reappeared in the hall.

“Is there a reason your bathroom is filled with moving boxes?”

Lena felt her face flush and rolled her eyes at herself. “Yes, there’s a reason; but not a very good one.”

Kara cocked her head to the side, grinning.

“You can uh, use the ensuite if you’d like.”

“Thanks. Maybe you can tell me about that reason when I get back?”

Lena nodded, before burying her head in the open fridge when Kara was out of sight. She desperately hoped she’d left nothing too incriminating out for her to see.

Five minutes later, they were both waiting in Lena’s kitchen as their meals reheated. Kara waited patiently at the counter, following the lines in the marble with her hands as their food rotated around in the microwave. Lena had originally planned on eating each of the four portions for lunch at work that week but supposed that wasn’t necessary anymore.

While she searched for clean knives and forks in her drawer, then the clean load in the dishwasher, Kara peered over at the page still pulled up on Lena’s laptop.

“Were you looking up info on those aliens?” Kara asked.

With her body hunched over the dishwasher, Lena misread Kara’s tone and sprang back up with an apology on her tongue. Kara’s expression made her take pause, though. She looked excited, if not a tad bashful.

“I did look into it a bit,” Lena said, setting down one set of silverware for Kara and one for herself. “The correspondents on the news were spouting outright rubbish, so I thought I’d look into it myself.”

Kara beamed at her. “And what did reporter Lena figure out might I ask?”

Lena sighed as she placed their dinners—reheated lasagna with an extra portion of meat sauce for Kara—down on the counter.

“Not a lot. There’s been more than one alien species documented that interacts with electrical components like that; however, I wasn’t sure if it was the actual wiring they could travel through or the microprocessor. Maybe both.”

“You think they’re like livewire?”

Lena hummed in confirmation. Over their food, Lena showed Kara the video clips she’d compiled of different meta-humans and aliens with similar abilities.

“I also found this forum from like two years ago where three users discussed potential power limitations of one of the aliens Superman fought. In every article the alien had been described as traveling through the grid, but these users speculated that the alien could only travel through components capable of data transfer. I tried to find out what happened with the alien or how Superman defeated them in the end, but there was nothing. If you’re still not able to track down the second alien or get any information from the one the DEO apprehended, it might be worth looking… What?”

Lena hit pause on the current video replaying the attack in Metropolis two years prior and turned to find Kara smirking.

Lena wrung her hands together as she said, “If I’m way off, please just disregard everything I said.”

Kara shook her head, but her smile never fell. Eventually, she laid her hand over Lena’s forearm and squeezed.

“You really are brilliant,” Kara said, eyes trained on Lena’s face.

“I’m—what?” Lena laughed, nervously.

“Brilliant,” Kara repeated.

She pulled Lena by the arm until they were facing each other in their barstools.

“Just in case you weren’t aware,” Kara added, almost at a whisper.

Lena’s heartbeat was so strong, she swore she could feel it pulsing against every spot her body made contact with—the counter pressed against her side, the seat under her butt, and most ardently, the skin beneath Kara’s fingers. Her gaze dropped from her intense blue eyes to Kara’s lips, then up again. On their journey back, Lena’s eyes caught on a red mark dotting her cheek.

“You—you have…”

Lena reached out and swiped her thumb across Kara’s cheek, coming away with a speck of sauce. Kara’s eyes tracked the movement the whole time.

“Tomato sauce,” Lena explained, wiping her thumb off on a napkin. “No idea how that got there.”

When Kara’s eyes, full of mirth, met Lena’s, they both curled forward in their chairs, laughing. Lena braced herself against the counter until her forehead found the cool material of Kara’s suit-clad shoulder. Instead of pulling back quickly, Lena let herself relax into Kara’s body. At the juncture of her suit and neck, Lena caught the scent of sweet perfume, mixed with the earthy undertones of the outdoors.

Lena breathed in and out slowly, watching as the fine hairs along Kara’s neck bristled under her attention.

“Lena?” Kara asked.

Lena hummed in response, catching the hitch in Kara’s breath as Lena expelled another warm breath from her lips.

“Do you—do you have any more of that lasagna?”

Lena sat back suddenly, pushing away the hand that had at some point settled against Lena’s upper back.

“Uh, yeah.”

She purposefully forced her gaze away from Kara’s face as she hopped down from the barstool. Kara tried reaching for her arm as she did so and connected with Lena’s side above her waist instead. Despite the multiple layers of clothing between them, all Lena could focus on as she reheated the last portions of dinner was how close those fingers had come to skimming her chest, and even more so, how pained she felt that it didn’t happen.

As Kara dug into her second helping of food, Lena retreated to her living room. She needed time to re-collect her thoughts and sitting a foot away from Kara was not the answer. With her head lying against the padded arm of her sofa, Lena distracted her tireless brain with the intermittent sounds of Kara tapping on her phone between bites of food.

 

She woke to a dark room hours later. Her heavy duvet had been kicked down to her shins and there was definitely a spot of drool on the pillow next to her face. Lena rolled over and found her phone plugged into the charger. She hadn’t taken the cardboard down from her windows yet, so it was only a bit disorientating to realize she’d slept past 8 a.m.

For a moment, she lied there motionless, wondering what part of the night before had been a dream and what had been real. Slowly it all came back to her—the sounds of Kara’s plate and cutlery clinking against the inside of her sink, the feel of her cool sheets sliding against her arms as Kara had moved her to bed (when she was once again mostly passed out), and the sound of the front door clicking shut.

She left.

Lena’s sleepy brain produced the thought, before her gaze flitted to the door to her room.

No.

The sound hadn’t been from last night. It’d been—

As if summoned from the thought alone, Kara poked her head into Lena’s room. She was still in her Supergirl suit and had reattached the cape.

“Mornin’,” she said, before stepping fully into the doorway.

Lena rubbed the sleep from her eyes and returned the greeting. It was still too soon after waking for her mouth to come up with its own words. Her nose on the other hand…

“Did you cook?”

Kara blushed, shaking her head no.

Lena started to pout (ever known to be a bit melodramatic in the morning) when Kara spoke up again.

“Believe me when I say that you should be thankful I didn’t. I did however pick up some croissants and coffees from this bakery on the corner. Cute place, although I’m pretty sure the older lady behind the counter was judging me.”

“For what?” Lena asked, finally sitting up in bed.

“She saw the two coffees and jumped to a few conclusions,” Kara replied.

Lena wasn’t sure why, but Kara’s face looked flushed, and now Kara wouldn’t even meet her eyes.

“Wait, what?”

What conclusions? What on Earth was she talking about?

During her own reflection, Kara started mumbling something, more to herself than in response to Lena.

“—and I don’t even know why that would be her first guess. Is Supergirl not the kind of person that would bring her coworkers coffee in the morning? That seems way more likely than some morning after paramour.”

“Kara?”

“Hmm?” 

“You brought food?” Lena prompted, trying her best to not latch onto any specific details of Kara’s rumination.

“Yes. And coffee!”

 

After freshening up in the bathroom, Lena walked into the kitchen to find Kara back at her designated barstool, plowing into what looked like a ham and cheese croissant.

“I got you one, but if you’d like another, I can save you more.”

Based on the number of flaky crumbs on her plate and the surrounding counter, Lena guessed it wasn’t Kara’s first.

“I would never dream of it,” Lena responded with a grin.

She sidled up to Kara, going for her own cup of coffee first.

“I knew I liked you for a reason,” Kara playfully nudged back.

By the time Kara was cleaning up her spot at the counter, Lena had finished half of her coffee and even nibbled on part of her croissant.

“I hate to run off on you like this, but I need to fly home and change, or I’m going to be seriously late,” Kara said, taking a final swig of her coffee.

Lena’s expression in return was blank.

 “You know… for work,” Kara added. “Unlike some people.”

“Wow. That was low.” Lena tried to scowl, but it felt physically impossible when basked in Kara’s light.

“Listen, I’m sorry about falling asleep on you last night,” Lena said, setting aside her breakfast for later when her stomach would hopefully have calmed down enough to properly digest the food. “I know it wasn’t much of a first date.”

Kara’s responding laugh was boisterous and full.

“Lena, that wasn’t our first date.”

“No?”

Kara leaned up against the counter next to Lena and shook her head.

“Oh, good.”

“Although…” Kara trailed off, hair falling across her face as her eyes traveled up Lena’s body. “If it was a first date, I could kiss you at the end.”

“Why can’t you now?”

“I—”

Her breath caught as Lena’s hand found purchase on the collar of her suit. Lena only had to tug once, before Kara was leaning in, closing the distance between them.

The kiss was chaste with the overwhelming bitter taste of coffee, yet it was still Lena’s favourite—for now. When Kara went back in, lips settling on either side of her bottom lip, Lena realized she was smiling wide and unyielding.

After exchanging another couple (or maybe more than a couple) kisses at the counter, Lena followed Kara to the front door to let her out.

“If you’re free for lunch, I could make up last night’s postponed date this afternoon,” Kara said, lingering in the hall.

Kara’s nervous disposition while wearing her Super suit was still a hell of a dichotomy for Lena to witness.

“As luck may have it, I am completely free this afternoon. Anywhere you had in mind? Please don’t say Wrapzone, though.”

“What! I thought you loved that place,” Kara replied, scandalized. 

“Kara, I worked the night shift. My only choices that late were Wrapzone or the Taco Mike’s on 3rd that closed at two.”

“Well, where would you like to go instead? Any preferences? I heard they opened a tapas bar across the street from that axe throwing place.”

Of course she knew where the axe throwing place was.

“I was thinking burgers, if you’re up for it.”

“If I’m up for it? Lena!” Kara guffawed, before settling back against the doorframe. “Burgers would be great.”

 

It was one thing to spend alone time with Kara when they were relaxing at Lena’s apartment late at night; however, being the woman’s sole focus in a bustling burger joint during lunch that afternoon was something Lena couldn’t have fully prepared for. For a superhero who quite possibly always kept one ear to the ground, she sure knew how to make Lena feel like the whole world.

Compared to a literal alien from another planet, Lena never considered herself to be particularly interesting, yet Kara remained fascinated by her stories.

“Do you ever see them anymore?” Kara asked.

They were waiting on their burgers and fries. Lena sat back on her side of the booth while Kara sipped slowly from her large vanilla shake. 

“I haven’t properly seen my mother since I finished school and Lex…” Lena leaned forward, searching absently for her bottle of water. “As well as we got on as kids, he’s different now. The last time he was out on bail he came by to visit. We went for drinks, and he took me to this exclusive bar.” Lena rolled her eyes at how pretentious the whole experience had been. “For the first hour it was familiar and comfortable and so nice to feel like I had another person on my team for once.”

Lena shook her head, trying the push that suffocating feeling away. It was like her throat was closing up—slowly constricting bit by bit with every long inhale. She would absolutely not, under any circumstances, start crying in the middle of a Big Belly Burger, though.

“After that, the conversation shifted away from easy things like work and how dysfunctional our mother could be, and he asked how Jack was doing these days. He was always a big fan of Jack, and sometimes I felt like he wanted Jack in my life more for himself than for my own happiness. I broke the news that I thought it was over and he did not take that well.”

When Lena looked back up to find Kara’s gaze, there was no judgement there. She simply nodded, waiting for Lena to continue.

“We’ve texted on occasion since, but his life feels so detached from my own now adays. It’s also difficult to chat about mundane things when I know that if he were anyone else or of any less societal standing, he’d be rotting in jail for at least twenty years.”

Lena sighed. A family of four had just walked in the side door by their booth, and Lena watched them pass as a self-pitying emotion swept through her—one that she hadn’t let affect her in months. When she looked back across the table, Kara’s shake had been abandoned, and she was leaning forward, chin resting in both her hands.

“Are you glad he’s not?” Kara asked.

“Glad he’s not in jail?” Lena huffed. “He’s a great shield from my mother, but I don’t know. Does it make me a bad person to not want someone who’s done egregious things to suffer for it?”

Kara let one of her hands drop from her face and reached out for Lena’s. She ran the pad of her thumb over the inside of Lena’s wrist, stopping when it met the silver chain of her watch.

“I think it’s harder to not wish suffering to all who have done wrong. When I’m you know who, I feel like I’m forced to look at the world in black and white, right and wrong, good and evil. People look to me to make these impossible decisions, and I’m just standing there wondering who made me the voice of reason when the only person I see in the mirror is a lost girl from a world that doesn’t exist anymore.

“So no, I don’t think I could ever see you, Lena Luthor, as a bad person.”

 

Once their food arrived, they shifted away from heavy topics, and instead talked about growing up in different parts of the country: adjusting to life in Midvale for Kara and life in the Luthor mansion for Lena. Kara told her stories about the first few years on Earth and the number of mishaps she’d experienced with her powers. Lena finally understood why Alex was so hellbent on having no more broken windows. Lena was more reserved when it came to life with the Luthors and the years she attended boarding school, only seeing her family on weekends. She didn’t shy away from any of her tales from university though—especially grad school. A few even managed to make Kara blush. 

 “Why are you looking at me like that?” Lena asked, taking advantage of Kara’s stunned silence to steal one of her fries. 

She had the feeling if Kara truly didn’t want to relinquish any, she wouldn’t, yet Lena still felt victorious as she dipped it in ketchup. 

“I don’t know,” Kara replied. 

She glanced down at her second burger and took a large bite. Lena clucked her tongue, sensing the obvious diversion. 

“Are people like me not allowed to be adventurous?”

Kara stared wide-eyed at her in return, slowly chewing her current bite, before swallowing. 

“I never said that,” she said, voice strained. “I just see you in your business-y clothes with your smarty-pants coding and atrociously healthy chicken wraps and—”

“—assumed I wasn’t the type?”

Kara scratched the side of her arm where she’d rolled up her professional, maroon button up before they’d dug into their food earlier. It was a little—scratch that—a lot distracting for Lena to continue teasing her when faced with the sheer amount of work the seams on Kara’s shirt were putting in. Frankly, the fact Kara wasn’t tearing through her little chinos and pastel cardigans like the Hulk on a daily basis was a feat in itself. 

“To tell you the truth,” Lena finally replied, “I wasn’t always the biggest fan of rave music.”

Kara’s nose scrunched in a way that was entirely too adorable. 

Lena chortled at the display, before continuing, “there was just something so alive about being in a sea of bodies that were moving to the same music, feeling the same energy, craving the same release. Plus, there were a lot of drugs—not like I approve now, but it was fun at the time.”

“I’m glad you enjoyed yourself,” Kara replied, struggling through the simple sentence like she’d just crossed the Sahara Desert on foot. 

Though they moved on from that conversation topic, Kara couldn’t seem to drop that shocked expression from her face. She mostly let Lena lead the conversation, only asking questions or adding in her two cents between bites of food. It gave Lena an addictive feeling at the thought that she could have this effect on the Girl of Steel. 

 

By the time Lena had finished her lunch and was walking Kara back to work, the high she’d been holding onto had faded, leaving a pleasant heaviness in her chest. A heaviness that told her this mattered, however seemingly inconsequential their conversations were.

“So did you always want to work in IT?” Kara asked, as she swung their arms between them back and forth.

At some point during their walk, her hand had found Lena’s and refused to relinquish its hold on her fingers.

“Did I always want to be some Big Brother at Catco?” Lena rephrased with a hint of astonishment in her voice.

Kara rolled her eyes. “Okay, yeah... Not like that. Just in general.”

Lena bit her lip and let her eyes roam over Kara’s face. Her profile was enchanting: pink lips, glowing cheeks, and wispy blonde eyebrows that looked translucent under the sun.

“I don’t think I have ever let myself answer that question without bias,” she eventually replied.

Kara turned; with her brows furrowed in concern, she cocked her head as if to ask why’s that?

Lena sighed and stilled their swinging arms at her side. They’d reached the block where the Catco building was. Throngs of people headed in and out of the main doors. So many that Dan from security might as well have shoved a doorjamb under both. Still, even being in the vicinity of her old job made her hesitant.

A squeeze of her hand from Kara forced Lena’s attention back to their conversation.

“Growing up, it was always about what I could bring to the Luthor name. Lex had a better business sense than me and though I loved science and math in general, my mother figured that a computer science degree would be most beneficial.”

“So, you didn’t even get a choice?”

Long before the Luthor drama overshadowed the small pleasures of her childhood, Lena loved to spend Sundays in her father’s office. Sunday was the only day of the week he refused to answer business calls. Instead, he devoted hours to reading in his armchair or listening to records all the way through. “Do you like this music?” he’d asked her one morning.

Life is what happens to you while you’re busy making other plans. For years she’d been so concerned with controlling every aspect of her life and planning ahead that she’d never been open to the unpredictable.

Kara was her unpredictable. Kara was life happening right in front of her.
